Talkativeness: Speaking far more than usual and continuing to talk without rest.\n  - Rapid Speech: Speech speed is so fast that it is difficult for others to understand or interrupt.\n  - Pressured Speech: Cannot stop talking or tolerate silence due to an internal pressure to keep speaking.\n  - Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli: Attention is immediately drawn to trivial and unimportant stimuli nearby (e.g., noise, movement).\n  - Frequent Task Switching: Unable to finish one task and constantly shifting attention to new things, starting multiple tasks simultaneously.\n  - Inability to Sustain Attention: Unable to focus on any activity for long, with attention constantly being scattered.\n  Duration: 4 months\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Emotional Numbness: A state where emotions feel dulled or paralyzed.\n  - Loss of Interest: Reduced interest in previously enjoyed hobbies or leisure activities.\n  - Social Withdrawal: Reduced interest or effort in social interactions and maintaining relationships.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Significant Weight Loss: Weight loss of more than 5% in a month without dieting.\n  - Weight Gain: Weight gain of more than 5% in a month.\n  - Decrease in Appetite: Decrease in appetite occurring nearly every day.\n  - Increase in Appetite: Increase in appetite occurring nearly every day.\n  - Chronic Fatigue: Fatigue that persists for a long period even after adequate rest.\n  - Physical Fatigue: A state of physical exhaustion where the body feels heavy and easily tires even from minor activities.\n  - I also feel constantly exhausted with absolutely no energy to do anything. I haven't really experienced thoughts of worthlessness or suicide, though.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting several days when you felt unusually energetic or euphoric, needed much less sleep, talked faster, or were more impulsive than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, I actually had a period of about four months where I felt abnormally energetic and euphoric. During that time, I barely needed any sleep but still felt completely refreshed, and I was talking much faster and more than usual.\n\nDoctor: During that four-month high-energy period, did you have any hallucinations or strongly held beliefs that others said were not true, like feeling especially powerful or chosen? I never felt unusually powerful or chosen, just very energetic.\n\nDoctor: During that four-month high-energy period, did your symptoms cause major problems like being hospitalized, getting into serious trouble, or being unable to work or function normally?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall having any problems as severe as being hospitalized or being completely unable to work during that period. I was still able to function and manage my daily life. It never really got to that extreme point."

This current nine-month period is actually the first time it has ever lasted this long.\n\nDoctor: During these nine months, have you also had symptoms like loss of interest or pleasure, changes in sleep or appetite, low energy, poor concentration, feelings of worthlessness, or thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've completely lost interest and pleasure in the things I used to enjoy, and my appetite has dropped significantly. I also feel constantly exhausted with absolutely no energy to do anything. I haven't really experienced thoughts of worthlessness or suicide, though.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting several days when you felt unusually energetic or euphoric, needed much less sleep, talked faster, or were more impulsive than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, I actually had a period of about four months where I felt abnormally energetic and euphoric. During that time, I barely needed any sleep but still felt completely refreshed, and I was talking much faster and more than usual.\n\nDoctor: During that four-month high-energy period, did you have any hallucinations or strongly held beliefs that others said were not true, like feeling especially powerful or chosen? Did those symptoms happen only during that mood episode?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't have any hallucinations or beliefs that others found untrue during that period. I never felt unusually powerful or chosen, just very energetic.\n\nDoctor: During that four-month high-energy period, did your symptoms cause major problems like being hospitalized, getting into serious trouble, or being unable to work or function normally?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall having any problems as severe as being hospitalized or being completely unable to work during that period. I was still able to function and manage my daily life. It never really got to that extreme point.\n\nDoctor: During that high-energy time, were those symptoms present most of the day nearly every day for at least 4 days in a row?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, absolutely. Those symptoms were there most of the day, nearly every single day. It actually lasted way longer than just four days, continuing pretty much non-stop for about four months.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are an assistant analyst connecting the [Symptom Profile] with the doctor's question.\nDetermine which part of the profile (chief complaint, associated symptoms, social history, etc.)\nthe doctor's **last question** is related to.\n\n[Symptom Profile]\n▸ hypomanic_episode\n  - Elevated Mood: A mood that is abnormally high, euphoric, and full of unmotivated happiness and vitality.\n  - Irritable Mood: A state where one gets angry or aggressive even at minor stimuli.\n  - Mood Lability: Rapid shifts in mood, alternating unpredictably between euphoria and irritability.\n  - Increased Energy: Overflowing physical and mental energy, leading to an inability to stay still and a desire to keep moving.\n  - Hyperactivity: Doing far more work than usual or starting multiple activities simultaneously.\n  - Psychomotor Agitation: Inability to tolerate resting or stopping, driven by a constant need to do something.\n  - Reduced Sleep Duration: Sleeping significantly less than usual but feeling refreshed rather than tired.\n  - Loss of Sleep Need: Not feeling the urge to sleep or the thought that sleep is necessary.\n  - Indifference to Insomnia: Feeling no problem and continuing activities despite staying up for several nights.\n  - Major Depressive Disorder is added as a differential because the patient clearly has a 9-month depressive episode, but it remains less likely unless the prior 4-month elevated period turns out not to meet full hypomanic criteria. Subtypes: Derailment or Loose Associations, Tangentiality, Incoherence or Word Salad, Neologisms, Clang Associations\n[S022] Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.  Subtypes: Childlike Silliness, Stupor, Catalepsy, Waxy Flexibility, Mutism, Negativism, Posturing, Mannerism, Stereotypy, Agitation, Grimacing, Echolalia, Echopraxia\n[S023] Negative_Symptoms: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.  Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists. Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.
